NEWSLETTER · 02 Nov 2026, 23:22

What A Day: Pool Boy Meets World

View this post on the web at https://crookedmedia.substack.com/p/what-a-day-pool-boy-meets-world

THE SITE OF THE SLIME Is Donald Trump trying to tank the midterms? He keeps handing the Democrats political gifts — including his bizarre bit today as America’s pool guy. Imagine posing next to the site of one of your most famously dunderheaded mistakes, some unforced error that earned you the mockery of an entire nation because it exposed your helpless incompetence. You draw yourself up proudly, point to your famous blunder and declare: This is why America should vote for my party. That was President Donald Trump today as he stood beside the Lincoln Memorial Reflecting Pool, the iconic monument that now reminds everyone this self-proclaimed real estate genius can’t even fix a pool. “We’ve done a great job here,” Trump said, falsely blaming “vandalism” for his own inability to renovate this basin for six months. Trump’s team spent millions trying to fix the landmark, struggling valiantly with an algae bloom that filled the basin and new waterproof coating that separated from the concrete bottom following his botched renovation. The Interior Department debunked Trump’s claims about vandals, citing faulty work by a contractor [ https://substack.com/redirect/acb047f0-3ab8-4af7-863d-6e3b8ee7856d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Weeks after arresting an alleged vandal, the Department of Justice dropped the charges [ https://substack.com/redirect/8dca40fb-39b2-4c17-ae1e-f1707bd7e127?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Even U.S. Attorney Jeanine Pirro said vandals [ https://substack.com/redirect/8dca40fb-39b2-4c17-ae1e-f1707bd7e127?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] aren’t to blame. But Trump won’t stop lying, claiming today that a “group of people slashed the hell out of it.” Today’s spectacle crystalizes how Trump put his own party in so much political peril in tomorrow’s elections, as if he were trying to tank the midterms. Famously immune to admitting defeat, Trump made the mind-boggling decision to return to the very spot of that grand embarrassment for his closing message in his midterm blitz. Trump apparently believes that hosting a rally at this venue could help his party. In reality, it’s further evidence that he’s out of touch with political reality — and unwilling to accept that he’s a major reason why Republicans are staring down a catastrophe. The latest polling is sparking panic on the right — as Democrats try not to get carried away with giddy optimism. For all the pomp and circumstance today, Trump is prepping excuses for defeat. “For some reason, the sitting president doesn’t win the midterms,” he said. “But we gave this a very strong shot.” I’d bet that he read the latest New York Times [ https://substack.com/redirect/44f5f65e-336c-403e-9d67-df37a527003c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]/Siena polls [ https://substack.com/redirect/44f5f65e-336c-403e-9d67-df37a527003c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], which everyone in politics can’t stop talking about. The results showed Democrats leading Republicans in seven Senate races in battleground states. (James Talarico is up 7 points in Texas. Texas!) Democrats need to flip four seats to win the Senate, and they’re already widely expected to win the House. “One year ago, when people asked me whether Democrats could win the Senate, I said we needed a perfect storm to have a chance,” Crooked’s Dan Pfeiffer wrote in his Message Box newsletter [ https://substack.com/redirect/700b873a-4be2-49d6-8585-9636c9187c51?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. “It [is] abundantly clear that the perfect storm has arrived.” The polls, of course, could be wrong. They have been before. But there are three strong reasons for Democrats to be confident this cycle: The Times adjusted its polling methodology to better represent conservative voters. More Americans disapprove of Trump than ever before [ https://substack.com/redirect/b13d0e3f-e6e1-4f7b-9fb7-9bfda999b752?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. And the GOP is running a slate of weak (or downright terrible [ https://substack.com/redirect/90fdc313-b145-49fc-92eb-f1819c27159d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]) candidates against strong Democratic hopefuls. And then there’s Trump’s half-hearted closing message. “The country has never done better,” he told reporters [ https://substack.com/redirect/c93e5b37-80c2-40d3-b08f-48c9461f6367?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] at his lavish Halloween party at Mar-a-Lago over the weekend. Does anyone actually believe that? WHAT ELSE? 👀 Michigan Democratic Senate candidate Abdul El-Sayed’s [ https://substack.com/redirect/d6b2ac49-1468-4185-a7f4-bbe13815e355?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] campaign said that it raised $2.4 million yesterday, its largest single-day haul on record. The influx of cash came after Rep. Haley Stevens (D-MI), who lost to him in the primary, stunned the country by withdrawing her support and instead endorsing former Rep. Mike Rogers, a Republican. “I’m at peace with this,” Stevens told a reporter [ https://substack.com/redirect/ec93a02c-e6b6-40a6-9156-56556a63de7f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] afterwards, offering a glass of bourbon and wearing fuzzy pink slippers. The Trump administration asked the Supreme Court [ https://substack.com/redirect/a7eecfaa-e22d-4f76-b645-5fa62b6c197f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to stop California from counting 20,000 alleged noncitizen ballots. A federal appeals court denied the request last week. Trump’s DOJ vowed to appeal a federal judge’s decision [ https://substack.com/redirect/7fe8ca85-e7b1-46c4-91c9-f6fd7103d5a2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to throw out a lawsuit to pre-emptively challenge the results of the Alaska Senate race if Democrat Mary Peltola wins. Trump’s team argued that the judge didn’t factor in the possibility that a so-called “spoiler” candidate with the same name as incumbent Sen. Dan Sullivan (R-AK) may have entered the race to confuse voters. (Counterpoint: Alaska uses ranked-choice voting, so anyone who is confused can simply rank both Dan Sullivans.) The average price of gas during Trump’s second term [ https://substack.com/redirect/394e8ada-9cb7-4bcb-bfec-2854c0c7e1bb?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] is expected to exceed that of any other president as of today, thanks to Trump’s war in Iran. Iranian leaders offered to open the Strait of Hormuz within two days if the Trump administration agrees to demands including sanctions relief and the release of frozen assets, which Trump’s team has previously been unwilling to do. Ukraine accused Russia of using the Melania Trump-brokered [ https://substack.com/redirect/ace0961f-e6af-4710-94b7-14ece474479e?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] ceasefire in a small town to rearm troops and strengthen its military positions. Kremlin spokesperson Dmitry Peskov offered this response: “This is standard practice in such situations. But I’m not familiar with the specifics.” Trump’s Qatari Air Force One flew as low as 400 feet [ https://substack.com/redirect/722af6c7-108c-464b-aed1-1cdf97f81f84?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] over the nation’s capital as part of a stunt for a Halloween party on the White House lawn last week, according to flight data. To put that into perspective, the Washington Monument is 555 feet tall. Trump vowed to “protect” tech bro Elon Musk if Democrats [ https://substack.com/redirect/1c0c5cb3-0974-4a87-9bd1-ca3de4a5cd46?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] win power in the midterms and investigate the gazillionaire. The president also said that he’d consider pre-emptive pardons for his staffers and other allies, if Democrats come after them. Musk is trying to launch his own mobile phone company [ https://substack.com/redirect/784c613b-17c9-4ab8-bc8a-b6bb0d07e545?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] through SpaceX, which has recently had talks with Boost Mobile to acquire or use its network, the Washington Post reports. Verizon, T-Mobile and AT&T had turned down Musk’s request to use their networks, amid fears that he might threaten the industry. LIGHT AT THE END… ☀️ A federal judge dismissed the Trump DOJ’s complaint [ https://substack.com/redirect/0eeaaf7d-5046-4dfd-bb9f-8f435c7a21b3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] against two former federal agents who had been accused of downplaying their knowledge of the Hunter Biden laptop scandal while testifying in front of Congress. Trump’s DOJ just can’t stop losing! Four NASA crewmates emerged from a chamber [ https://substack.com/redirect/2e130e7e-05d7-47fd-952a-19ca3154d1f4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] that simulated a mission to Mars — after 378 days. Since last October, the crew had been living in a 3D-printed habitat where they grew plants with soil that’s supposed to mimic what’s on Mars, and communicated with NASA officials just as they would if they were 140 million miles away. (I wish they could’ve put me in this thing beginning January 20, 2025. Sounds kinda relaxing, tbh.) Scottish police arrested a man who allegedly [ https://substack.com/redirect/3980e8d1-9208-4fff-ac8c-b2289b1d912c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] had drugs in his car… and discovered a lamb named Pablo eating a bag of french fries in the back seat. Pablo was eventually returned to his owners and is in good health, though I would recommend he eat carrots, lettuce, or whatever lambs eat instead of fast food. Runners high-fived NYC Mayor Zohran Mamdani as he handed out [ https://substack.com/redirect/461aac1b-36b4-4e21-8399-8831017aa7cb?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] water around the 13th mile of the New York City marathon over the weekend. When one runner stopped and told him she was getting married in Central Park, he responded [ https://substack.com/redirect/3000fdd3-1b8d-4c2c-82aa-cc3a1126414a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]: “OH MY GOD CONGRATULATIONS!” A Florida woman became a local celebrity after [ https://substack.com/redirect/54439ffc-7797-4acc-830b-cb7f8b258237?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] a bar’s surveillance footage caught her stopping on her way to a Halloween party while wearing a banana costume to check on a driver who had crashed his car. The bar named a cocktail after the woman, made of rum and banana, in her honor. This Substack is reader-supported. Expert Q&A

Jane Nakano

Jane Nakano is a senior fellow in the Energy Security and Climate Change Program at the Center for Strategic and International Studies (CSIS). Her research interests include U.S. energy policy; energy geopolitics; global market and policy developments concerning natural gas, hydrogen, nuclear energy, and critical minerals; and energy and climate issues in the Asia-Pacific region.

Read full bio →

Q: What major shifts in energy policy have you observed across Asian countries in response to this year’s disruptions to global energy trade?

There is a heightened focus on oil reserves. Many Asian economies outside Northeast Asia’s advanced economies lack a meaningful oil supply buffer, and are increasingly motivated to build national reserves and pursue regional cooperation, such as joint stockpiling. Also, the regional interest in clean energy technologies is increasingly driven by the desire to reduce dependence on fuel imports rather than to mitigate CO2 emissions.

Q: How have energy policy priorities evolved across East and Southeast Asia over the past five years, and where have you observed the greatest convergence or divergence?

The turbulence in global oil and gas markets since 2022 and the general electrification trend, now accentuated by the strong demand outlook from data center ambitions, are making the security of electricity supply a top policy priority across Asia. How to meet this challenge is where divergence emerges. Multilateral power trade has risen as a strategic priority for Southeast Asian governments (i.e., the ASEAN Power Grid initiative) while nuclear energy has become more politically viable across East Asia.

Read Jane's insights

AT THE SCENE

Témiscaming, Canada

The town threatened by Trump's tariffs

Workers hope to mobilise a local coalition to buy the plant back from US-based RYAM, which purchased it in 2017.

A town in Quebec that was built over a century ago around a paper mill is now fighting for its future. Most of the mill's 360 employees are out of work, after President Donald Trump imposed more tariffs on Canada following the breakdown of trade talks.

Jessica Murphy, Canada digital editor and Nadine Yousif, Senior Canada reporter

Around Témiscaming, "For Sale" signs dot the yards of modest middle-class homes. "If the mill goes down, it'll turn into a ghost town," says Ronnie Raymond, one of the many locals who come from a family of mill workers. "There'll be nothing, nothing left. There's no work for anybody, really. It's scary."

Many workers in the town say they are placing some hope in the upcoming US midterm elections to put pressure on Trump to ease some of his tariffs on Canada. "We'll take any help we can get," resident Nadine Suley says, adding: "There are Americans that care what's going on, and it's affecting them too." There are signs the Trump administration is taking note of that pressure.

BEYOND THE HEADLINES

The 'one-woman army' taking on the Iranian regime

Samira says she will 'fight the Islamic Republic until my last breath. Credit: Sisu Pictures Ltd

Samira's 16-year-old daughter Sevda was shot dead by Iranian security forces in front of her during mass protests against the regime in January. Samira then fled the country and is now in hiding abroad, where she is searching for justice after her daughter's death, naming online the officials she believes were responsible. Despite the risks, Samira says she refuses to be silenced. The Iranian embassy in London told us that peaceful protests had been hijacked by foreign powers which promoted violence.

NEWSLETTER · 02 Nov 2026, 18:54

Thousands March Against ‘Vote Theft’, Unmindful of Toxic Delhi Haze; Bombay HC Gives Relief to Actor Imran Khan Bu…

View this post on the web at https://www.theindiacable.com/p/thousands-march-against-vote-theft

A newsletter from The Wire | Founded by MK Venu, Pratik Kanjilal, Sidharth Bhatia, Siddharth Varadarajan, Sushant Singh, Seema Chishti, and Tanweer Alam | Contributing writers: Kalrav Joshi, Anirudh SK If you like our work and want to support us, then do subscribe. To take out a fresh paid subscription or to renew your existing monthly or annual subscription, please click on the special payment page we have created – https://rzp.io/rzp/the-india-cable [ https://substack.com/redirect/11bf2bfb-013b-4417-93fc-43b0439afc65?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Editor’s note: This post may be cut off in your inbox due to length. Click on the title above to read it in full on Substack. Snapshot of the day November 2, 2026 Sidharth Bhatia, Kalrav Joshi, Anirudh S.K. The fact that a noxious haze blocked out Delhi’s afternoon sun yesterday proved no bar for thousands of citizens, who marched along Minto Road into Ramlila Maidan for the INDIA bloc’s ‘Vote Bachao, Desh Bachao’ rally demanding chief election commissioner Gyanesh Kumar’s resignation. Though the Congress and other opposition parties have faced criticism over the last several years for failing to mobilise their cadre, yesterday’s Ramlila rally witnessed the largest such mobilisations by a united opposition in recent memory [ https://substack.com/redirect/23c3627d-ceca-4734-8441-1870eb8c4dec?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], reports Sravasti Dasgupta, noting the prominent participation not just of the grand old party but also the Samajwadi Party, the Rashtriya Janata Dal and the Left parties. Lok Sabha leader of opposition Rahul Gandhi said [ https://substack.com/redirect/d8a56483-3cce-4af7-87e6-3dc188580a99?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] during the rally that CEC Kumar has “committed the biggest crime” of “[trying] to shut off the country’s voice and its soul” and that for the opposition, ensuring that citizens retain their voice is “even more important than religion” [ https://substack.com/redirect/72e3e55f-4f1b-4e34-9088-9fc0d23ad429?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. If the visuals were anything to go by, Ramlila Maidan was a sea of flags. And the rally’s message went well beyond alleged vote theft. “Beyond alleged electoral irregularities, protesters raised issues ranging from unemployment, inflation to women’s safety and the rights of marginalised communities,” reports [ https://substack.com/redirect/3c29fe9a-13da-4cdb-b021-83fcc4fab9a2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]Deccan Herald [ https://substack.com/redirect/3c29fe9a-13da-4cdb-b021-83fcc4fab9a2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. [See Reportedly] For its part the BJP said the rally was a “complete waste of time”. Home minister Amit Shah, who was in Chandigarh for a police event, accused the Congress [ https://substack.com/redirect/23c3627d-ceca-4734-8441-1870eb8c4dec?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] of rubbing salt into the wounds of Sikhs by holding the event on November 1, around the 42nd anniversary of the 1984 anti-Sikh pogroms. “Orders were issued from abroad to hold the rally on the very day that thousands of Sikhs were burned alive,” Shah said. On the same day that the Commission for Air Quality Management imposed Stage 2 restrictions under the Graded Response Action Plan in Delhi and the NCR [ https://substack.com/redirect/312cf244-59e5-42c0-a94b-5fbe66a8d932?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], the Supreme Court allowed the manufacture, sale and bursting of certified green firecrackers for two days during Diwali [ https://substack.com/redirect/0e549ced-05a1-4d13-b6b5-189210f2e0f6?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. A bench of Justices MM Sundresh and PB Varale permitted their use between 6 am and 8 pm on November 7, and between 7 am and 10 pm on November 8. The complete ban on firecrackers will be restored from November 9, the bench added. Actor Imran Khan won’t have to quit India just yet because the Bombay high court today extended its stay of the government’s order that he leave the country in retaliation for his attending the October 10 protest against the CEC in Mumbai. But the court said that Khan, being a US national with an overseas citizen of India status, cannot claim the same rights as Indian citizens [ https://substack.com/redirect/ef19bc4c-34a6-4744-bea4-c9e128bb82a5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. It also directed him to offer an unconditional apology as well as an undertaking that he will not participate in more protests. Khan also had to be given an opportunity to respond to the government’s notice. Samajwadi Party leader Azam Khan on Monday moved the Supreme Court...

2 November, 2026

Lily Jamali

North America tech correspondent

Greetings from San Francisco. Americans head to the polls on Tuesday. Here in California, voters will decide whether to levy a special one-time tax on billionaires - a potential 'world first', if passed. But the tactics that tech titans have deployed to fight it have proved controversial. More on that below.

Plus, as Binance users withdraw some $3.6bn (£2.7bn) after the largest-ever crypto hack https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDgsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABsZ0Wc22b4CZ0sN8NjX0LNySc0khUdukIDs0QjrDcBOy_AMT41uIljjop5xZnXkU2SkZ1S1cHOTcOBKhg2AF2AnY0jl0mYASGpV0WIiy , read my report on who the prime suspects are. Meanwhile, both Russians and Ukrainians have been wrongly warned of impending attacks - because of hacks. And we have some video game nostalgia.

THE DOWNLOAD

Taxing the titans

https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDgsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABsZ0Wc23xgnKZw8cNpMRarxuBdNmZ-nbxBu6tzHJy16QOHJejMv6no2-tOpgJJMjYFXAyq_bNTaqTnpWcQ5GU7jKH9Vj7btaAR4UNlHr California is home to the likes of Mark Zuckerberg, Kim Kardashian and Jensen Huang. Credit: Reuters

About 200 billionaires live in California - and many of them are going to extraordinary lengths to protect themselves from Proposition 40.

The measure would place a one-time 5% tax on the net worth of each of them

https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDgsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABsZ0Wc24V9EweSKkKkVok7HjOuuuyD_HzJexzENYxolB5V1AUQeeojTvoXKt0p12oRBw8NyW-mdnLhkWMFZEIWg_Dz3I2Uro1vh-HXSH to help fund healthcare and education. Federal money for state social services have been cut under President Donald Trump, while the wealthiest Americans have benefited from his policies.

Opponents say Proposition 40 will drive out the richest residents - and their dollars. Some proponents say 'good riddance', while others argue the billionaires could improve public services for millions if they stay.

Google co-founder Sergey Brin moved across the border to Nevada late last year. Meta boss Mark Zuckerberg recently got the Instagram owner's board to agree on a deal https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDgsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABsZ0Wc258_PHmS3LhArgoAhmKh21Y0LskKnC7YhHPA7Z6weBCHHDpAmo6yCFEpPIGw6fVtMrvSZWHzq4vWFGGpEeoN7mdjcuGyG5LBJN that makes it easier for him to pay his expected tax bill and stay in the state.

And then there's the flood of cash. A chunk of the state's billionaire class has poured about $300m into blocking the plan, local reports suggest. California's wealthiest have likely spent more on this than on any political cause in state history, according to Politico.

The most recent polling shows the measure's passage is far from guaranteed. Of course, not all of California's billionaires are tech leaders. But the fact that the idea has even made it to the ballot demonstrates the limits on the power of Silicon Valley's tech luminaries - many of whom were once celebrated as icons.

The download: _The current political climate has eroded public goodwill towards tech leaders. On Tuesday, California voters will give us an idea of by how much._

NEWSLETTER · 02 Nov 2026, 16:35

Blue Texas Is a Mirage—Until It Isn’t

View this post on the web at https://www.thebulwark.com/p/blue-texas-is-a-mirage-until-it-isnt-james-talarico-ken-paxton-senate-trump-vance-midterms

Weatherford, Texas “It’s not the heat, it’s the humidity.” When used to describe the outdoors, this phrase is scary enough. It conjures up visions of stagnant, swampy air, sweat that won’t evaporate. But I had never really considered what it meant when applied indoors. I’d find out in Weatherford, as hundreds crowded into an un-air-conditioned warehouse area at the recently erected JRJ Construction building. Turns out it’s neither the heat nor the humidity that gets you, but the smell, a sort of moldering fetidity that lingers just above the crowd like smog. It had been a journey to get inside the room: ninety minutes and some $40 of tolls headed west from Dallas to a few miles outside Fort Worth, followed by shuttle buses from the parking lot at the Parker County Sheriff’s Posse Rodeo Grounds & Event Center to JRJ, where I joined a winding line that took an hour before passing through the metal detectors. Rather than zip in with members of the press, I had signed up for general-admission tickets—hey, I’m a Texas resident; maybe my vote could be swayed—and so got to mingle with my fellow voters before entering. It was a bigger crowd than I expected, not huge by the standards of presidential rallies—but then, the president wasn’t here, and we were well over a thousand people strong for Trump’s backup act [ https://substack.com/redirect/bebf9835-1bb8-4555-9ab1-36b226d8d698?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. I was a little surprised so many people had shown up to this rural locale, as it was the eighth time that either Trump or Vance has been in Texas for a midterm rally. And that’s all since the nearly unprecedented midterm convention in Dallas back in September [ https://substack.com/redirect/bc801051-4c3e-4853-8831-bf0b980e9593?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Unable to handle the smog, I stepped outside to grab a bite (a pleasingly overstuffed chopped-brisket sandwich from the Tailgaters BBQ food truck, $13) and contemplate just how we got to the point where Texas is a genuine battleground state—at least for one cycle. Walking past the captive audience was a pair of merch vendors pushing carts full of garish Trump gear. (The highlight: an item that appeared to be socks emblazoned with Trump’s face and topped with a fringe of orange hair.) “Don’t have a Trump hat, you’re pretty much a Democrat,” one of the barkers intoned. Judging by the crowd, that seemed about right. Surprisingly, when I asked one of the swag sellers later what the most popular hat was, she said it was the pink fuzzy one. Men and women alike in Texas went nuts for them: “It’s different in Texas.” It struck me as an amusingly incongruous statement, given the tenor of the afternoon’s event, which featured speaker after speaker warning that James “Talafreako” was going to make your kids trans before forcing them to get gay-married to prove to the rest of the country that Texas could be made to submit to the liberal agenda. I am only barely exaggerating. Jace Yarborough, the Republican candidate in Texas’s 32nd Congressional District, insisted that no one in the crowd would apologize for fighting “trans ideology.” Mays Middleton, running for state attorney general, insisted that “we gotta protect our kids from people like James Talarico.” Another politician, whose name I unfortunately missed but have chosen to think of as Rep. Grinds My Gears (R-Tex.), enumerated his “tired-of” list. Among the things he’s tired of: “the transgenders” and “Bibles being taken off shelves.” JD Vance took the high road and instead focused solely on the administration’s many accomplishments. Wait, sorry, I’m checking my notes: Vance joked that Talarico doesn’t need to grapple with his masculinity because “there ain’t a lot to grapple with” and that “Talafreako” was handpicked by devious Washington, D.C., suits because they thought he was more electable despite “talking about his favorite drag queens or how there are six genders or about how whiteness is a disease.” The rally put into stark relief the terms of the desperate Republican effort to stave off a generational defeat in Texas: It’s a campaign designed to stir fear and loathing, centered on a phantom menace. Texas Republicans’ biggest problem is that the largest Democratic constituency in the state has something real to fear, and it’s driving a huge surge in turnout. MUCH WAS MADE OF THE SHIFT of the Hispanic vote toward Donald Trump in 2024, where some exit polls showed him capturing as much as 55 percent of the vote [ https://substack.com/redirect/3bca3384-8ade-40f9-86f9-5414afd424ba?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. It was one of those swings that could be considered surprising but not shocking, as the Texas–Mexico border was a complete disaster during much of the Biden administration, with waves of undocumented immigrants and phony asylum seekers washing up on the shores of border towns. This led to increases in crime and decreases in feelings of safety and strained local public resources. Legal immigrants and their families were mad, and wanted someone who would secure the border. What they didn’t count on was an administration that secured the border and then weaponized ICE against American citizens and otherwise law-abiding illegal immigrants alike. “It’s really scary here,” said Jackie Rosa, a Democratic strategist who is working on Bobby Pulido campaign in Texas’s 15th district, where the Tejano singer has built a shocking and consistent polling lead over incumbent Republican Monica De La Cruz. “Regardless of citizenship status, if you’re Hispanic here, you’re seeing lawless ICE detentions. Even citizens, people who are legally allowed to be here, like, it’s scary for young people.” As my colleague Adrian Carrasquillo has chronicled [ https://substack.com/redirect/e7944b6f-62d0-49dc-9549-15e4a6bcecab?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] in his newsletter, Huddled Masses, ICE has been weaponized to a shocking extent, even being used to settle neighborhood disputes—that is, when masked agents of the state aren’t illegally stopping people [ https://substack.com/redirect/75afb163-96a8-4bc9-8dc7-84c646cb1836?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] or executing protesters in the streets [ https://substack.com/redirect/0eafeb19-e068-443b-b6af-341c5fbdf05b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Even those who felt that the border’s instability was a problem have watched in horror as ICE agents, desperate to meet deportation quotas set by head immigrant hunter Stephen Miller, started rounding up folks regardless of whether or not they were causing public disorder. “They were promised that ICE would be going after criminals,” Rosa said of Hispanic voters in the south of Texas. “They believed that. Not, like, gardeners and grandmas or the workforce or even, you know, there’s been situations of citizens who have been detained here, and assaulted. It’s become really dark.” This concrete fear of masked agents of the state shanghaiing you to God-knows-where—a fear those of us of a certain age associate with the East German Stasi units—has undoubtedly goosed Hispanic turnout [ https://substack.com/redirect/df5bcc90-5fc0-40e1-a112-29674ce82ab4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], particularly among younger voters. And the GOP advantage with Hispanics has disappeared entirely, with recent polls showing Talarico beating Paxton by double digits [ https://substack.com/redirect/d2179df5-2dd5-4927-a4ac-d8e647a77620?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] among Latino voters. As of this past weekend, more than 760,000 Texans who hadn’t voted at all in 2024 have reportedly voted early, and nearly half of them are Hispanic [ https://substack.com/redirect/b9f3b8a3-6714-4ecb-892e-efd0be16ac44?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. No wonder Paxton has, apparently, written off the southern part of the state, canceling events in El Paso and, ironically, at the Alamo so he can focus on turning out the vote in the northern part of the state. It’s clear that Republicans think they’re in trouble. At the rally in Weatherford, state comptroller Don Huffines—who vaguely resembles a real-life version of one of the puppets from the 1980s sketch show Spitting Image [ https://substack.com/redirect/937c98ec-ef32-460c-b257-3972d848edcc?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]—tried to convey the deficit Republicans are facing after several days of early voting, noting that Democrats outnumbered Republicans by 600,000 votes. REPUBLICAN FEAR IN TEXAS on Election Day is unusual because Texas is an unusually red state. It’s huge and it’s maroon, cabernet-red. When The Bulwark hosted a live event in 2024 in Dallas [ https://substack.com/redirect/f622c3f4-7a9e-4149-b934-1f933d58cbdd?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], a member of the audience asked how to turn the state blue, and my suggestion was to not try and spend the resources elsewhere. This was not a popular answer, as you might imagine, but it’s the same answer I likely would’ve given in 2018 when Beto O’Rourke was making his run, or in 2020, when Trump’s popularity cratered in the face of the pandemic and an onrushing global economic catastrophe. But in 2026, the vibes have shifted; there’s real reason for Democratic hope and Republican concern. If you want to understand why, it’s because...

Open Letters, from Anne Applebaum

1 item in this evidence packet.

NEWSLETTER · 02 Nov 2026, 14:13

American Bullying

View this post on the web at https://anneapplebaum.substack.com/p/american-bullying

In at least three European countries, the Trump administration has tried over the past year to to influence the composition of government coalitions. In Poland the intervention was direct: the president asked one of the party leaders to switch sides. In Romania, pressure came from Kimberly Guilfoyle, until recently the US ambassador to Greece, who allegedly had commercial interests in changing the Romanian government (and bragged about her abilities to do so at a dinner party in Athens). In Germany, pressure takes the form of hints and suggestions that the Christian Democrats (CDU) should drop their social democratic partners and do a deal with the Alternative for Germany (AfD), a far-right, pro-Russian party. I wrote about these efforts in the Atlantic [ https://substack.com/redirect/6f1412d5-2e80-4f09-9f89-aa11936f1a60?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] (and I am sending it out via Substack right away, because it may be the last time to read about European politics before the midterms swamp all other news…) Here is the central argument: Americans once promoted democracy in Europe as a political system, and under that general understanding accepted all elected politicians of the center-left and center-right as legitimate. This administration and its adjacent business elite are now pursuing a different policy. In some cases, they promote the interests of parties that they believe to be ideologically aligned with the MAGA movement, including some that are openly pro-Russian. Other European political figures seem to have gained Washington’s blessing (or are trying to gain it) because they can promote commercial interests favored by someone close to the administration. In at least three cases, Americans have reportedly tried to persuade elected European leaders to dump their current coalition partners in favor of new ones more to the White House’s liking. The article has the details [ https://substack.com/redirect/6f1412d5-2e80-4f09-9f89-aa11936f1a60?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], which are shocking enough. But there is also another aspect to the story: On the one hand, American officials encourage Europeans to build up their own military forces. Speaking at NATO headquarters last summer, Elbridge Colby, an undersecretary of defense, said [ https://substack.com/redirect/92cb3186-0fc7-4949-b0fb-e4548a416272?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] that in the future, American allies should be “taking primary responsibility for Europe’s conventional defense.” Germany, Poland, and Romania are all countries that have taken this request seriously. But on the other hand, the same administration is directly promoting the fortunes of political parties and leaders who oppose defense spending, who favor Russia, or who do not promise to defend Europe or respect European borders. Alice Weidel, AfD’s co-leader, opposes [ https://substack.com/redirect/871eec82-16e7-4f1f-8c0d-335df0dc773c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] offering military aid to Ukraine and also argues against defense cooperation with other European countries. The party’s co-chair Tino Chrupalla has called [ https://substack.com/redirect/2b023086-173d-4ac3-b028-ef5c10709070?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] for the expulsion of American troops from Germany. George Simion, the leader of the Romanian far right, has in the past advocated for the restoration of Romania’s pre-1940 borders, including territory now in Ukraine. The Polish far right has lately begun repeating anti-European and anti-Ukrainian narratives of a kind that are now popular in both Washington and Moscow, undermining support for a unified European defense. In effect, the administration is promoting European rearmament and European defense cooperation while simultaneously promoting politicians who oppose rearmament or defense cooperation. Across democratic Europe, the public and the politicians are confused. Once, Europeans generally believed that America promoted democracy—the political system, not particular parties—and staunchly opposed Russia and other authoritarian regimes. Now, it seems, Trump-administration officials have flipped the script. What is it, exactly, that the administration wants? Rearm Europe, and then hand it over to nationalists? We’ve seen that scenario before, in the 20th century, and it ended really badly. The good news, if you are American, you can oppose these policies tomorrow, by collecting one of these stickers: One election won’t fix the whole problem, but it’s a start. Nikiszowiec Built for coal miners and their families in the early 20th century, before the First World War, Nikiszowiec, now a neighborhood in Katowice, southern Poland, was intended to be a self-contained community with gardens, schools, a communal laundry and shops. Now a historic district, Nikisiowiec is a testament to the fact that thoughtful architecture lasts. Open Letters, from Anne Applebaum is a reader-supported publication. To receive new posts and support my work, consider becoming a free or paid subscriber.

Unsubscribe https://substack.com/redirect/2/eyJlIjoiaHR0cHM6Ly9hbm5lYXBwbGViYXVtLnN1YnN0YWNrLmNvbS9hY3Rpb24vZGlzYWJsZV9lbWFpbD90b2tlbj1leUoxYzJWeVgybGtJam8wTVRnM01UYzNOamdzSW5CdmMzUmZhV1FpT2pJeU16RTFNalk0TXl3aWFXRjBJam94Tnprek5qSTFOVFl4TENKbGVIQWlPakU0TWpVeE5qRTFOakVzSW1semN5STZJbkIxWWkweU16VXhNelV6SWl3aWMzVmlJam9pWkdsellXSnNaVjlsYldGcGJDSjkuZjV6YnFmN0o5bWxDWEtsWHlCS01NMmVpaUVjSHIwNXRuTHZnSEZPSDVBMCIsInAiOjIyMzE1MjY4MywicyI6MjM1MTM1MywiZiI6dHJ1ZSwidSI6NDE4NzE3NzY4LCJpYXQiOjE3OTM2MjU1NjEsImV4cCI6MjEwOTIwMTU2MSwiaXNzIjoicHViLTAiLCJzdWIiOiJsaW5rLXJlZGlyZWN0In0.9rymZRTk0wrJLmkdAEDOyepZnkOrJ6Z9ohXSA4M7XI4?

FP’s Ravi Agrawal

We are understanding the situation," and added, "I don't know if North Korea is behind it, but if so, I will talk with North Korea about this issue." Earlier that day, the Federal Bureau of Investigation (FBI) pointed to North Korean hacking group "TraderTraitor" as the culprit behind the Binance Holdings Ltd. hack, in which more than $6 billion (about 8 trillion won) in cryptocurrency was stolen.

America’s AI Ambitions Depend on Taiwan

America’s AI Ambitions Depend on Taiwan. The United States and China are locked in a global competition to determine who will dominate artificial intelligence and whether it will be democracies or autocracies that set the standards and values of the new digital age. The United States leads the world in the sophistication and power of its artificial intelligence models, and its innovation base and investment in research and development remain unparalleled.But AI competition is much broader than modeling alone. It encompasses a technology ecosystem that includes power generation, servers, and deployment in real-world applications. Jensen Huang, co-founder and CEO of Nvidia, has described the AI industry as a five-layer cake with energy at its base and chips, infrastructure, models, and applications above it. To win the AI race with China, the United States needs to maintain or establish an advantage in each of these layers. And to do that, the United States needs Taiwan.

The Guardian view on Poland’s political turmoil: an ominous portent of bigger battles to come. Editorial

The Guardian view on Poland’s political turmoil: an ominous portent of bigger battles to come. Editorial. . Donald Tusk is attempting to restore legitimacy to the country’s constitutional court. In a polarised country and continent, that carries political risks . The Polish prime minister, Donald Tusk, must occasionally look towards Budapest and sigh. A landslide victory in elections last spring enabled Hungary’s new prime minister, Péter Magyar, to act with ruthless determination to clean up the country’s politics after 16 years of corrupt Orbánisation Mr Tusk, whose centrist coalition ousted the far-right Law and Justice party (Pi S) in 2023, is similarly taking on the legacy of a government which undermined the rule of law and democratic norms. But his efforts have been relentlessly boxed in by the blocking tactics of Poland’s (Pi S)‑backed president, Karol Nawrocki. At the weekend, this standoff escalated into a crisis with wider implications for the European Union as well as Poland itself. Exasperated by Mr Nawrocki’s attempts to veto the appointment of new judges to the country’s constitutional court – which has been illegitimately dominated since 2015 by Pi S‑friendly appointees – Mr Tusk backed the enforced appointment of a new head, and the suspension of the court’s president and vice‑president. Extraordinary scenes followed, as rightwing demonstrators massed outside the court building in the early hours of Saturday morning and Mr Nawrocki pushed past security forces to protest. A decade of magical thinking since Brexit has taught me that it’s always OK to hope for better. Zoe Williams

A decade of magical thinking since Brexit has taught me that it’s always OK to hope for better. Zoe Williams. . The world has changed enormously since 2016, yet the pro-Brexiters believe remainers like me have no chance of achieving a closer relationship with the EU. I’m choosing to ignore them . However sunny your disposition, it wears you down, 10 straight years of being asked to accept facts about Brexit that aren’t a flaky, half-baked referendum as the endpoint of purest democracy; the severance of a complicated trading and geopolitical relationship that took decades to build as the dawn of a new prosperity. All such nonsense, all delivered with such conviction, by such a wide variety of sources, many of them self-describing as impartial, that in the end you just surrender to it. None of it magically becomes true, don’t be silly. It’s more that you come to accept that everyone else talks nonsense now. Nobody get too excited – just because Andy Burnham has reopened a debate that upside-down world said couldn’t be reopened, it doesn’t mean the UK is on the brink of coming to its senses. I have no idea what’s going to happen; all I can say for certain is that times have changed more than I realised. The pro-Brexit crew have updated their arguments. They no longer say leaving the EU will bring untold riches, and they never even namecheck all the control we were meant to have taken back. They rarely mention any immigration benefits because that unwieldy issue now has a life of its own – or, to put that more simply, it turns out that in or out of the European Union, we can still fight about our borders like rats in a sack. Article: Systemic Approach to Memory, Knowledge, and Context in Agentic AI Architectures. . It separates context, memory, and knowledge, and recommends tiered memory, curated knowledge, and token budgeting.
